A useful first pass should take around 25 to 40 minutes. If the goal is toolchain adoption, verify three things: whether the comparison to GPTQ is strictly apples-to-apples, whether the method needs extra calibration cost, and whether it really behaves as a drop-in replacement across pipelines. If the goal is research follow-up, the natural question is whether the same ADMM formulation can be extended to activation quantization, KV-cache quantization, or jointly optimized with rotation/scaling transforms.35:T736,The method has three core pieces. First, a task-agnostic action-conditioned video world model. The implementation is built on WAN 2.1, but the important design choice is the training constraint: the world model is trained only on broad, target-task-independent interaction data such as play data or non-overlapping benchmark tasks. The goal is to learn transferable physical dynamics rather than task templates.

Second, zero-shot reward from a frozen VLM. Instead of training a reward model for each task, the method feeds imagined rollout videos and the language instruction into Qwen3-VL and asks for binary success judgments. This removes the need for task-specific reward supervision. The trade-off is clear: less reward shaping and less task specialization, in exchange for much better reuse across tasks.

Third, policy optimization inside the frozen world model. The policy backbone is OpenVLA-OFT. It is first lightly supervised with a few demonstrations to bind the policy to the new task semantics, then optimized with GRPO entirely in imagination. Because unseen-task rollouts from a generative world model can produce hallucinated successes, the paper adds a dual-noise verification mechanism: generate rollouts under different diffusion noise seeds from the same initial condition, and accept the reward only when the VLM judgment is consistent across them. This is a targeted defense against reward hacking induced by world-model hallucination. A success that appears only under one noise realization is treated as unreliable.

The novelty is therefore mostly in the system design and the constraints, not in inventing a new VLA, VLM, or video diffusion model. RAW-Dream turns existing components into a strictly task-agnostic post-training loop and adds the minimum extra machinery needed to make that loop stable on unseen tasks.36:T62a,The evaluation covers both simulation and real robots. In simulation, the world model is trained only on LIBERO-90 data, while four held-out suites—Spatial, Object, Goal, and Long—are used as downstream tasks that are completely unseen during world-model training. This is a stronger test than the usual low-data adaptation setting because the simulator has zero exposure to the target tasks. RAW-Dream improves over a 1-shot SFT baseline by +8.9% and outperforms online RL despite the latter using 50x more real-world data. That comparison is important: the method is not merely data-efficient at the cost of weak performance; it is competitive in a regime where real interaction is expensive.

On real robots, the world model is pretrained only on diverse, uncurated play data, again without target-task rollouts. Each new task starts from a 3-shot SFT policy and is then refined through imagined RL. The reported gain is +21.7% absolute success rate over the 3-shot SFT baseline. For robotic manipulation, that is a large enough margin to show that the imagined training signal is doing real work rather than producing simulator-only improvements.

The most important ablations appear to be: whether a task-agnostic world model can support unseen-task adaptation at all, whether zero-shot VLM reward is reliable enough to drive RL, and whether dual-noise verification reduces hallucinated success and false-positive reward hacking. Even without every table reproduced here, the paper’s empirical case depends on this combination rather than on a single headline number.37:T4f5,Start with the problem definition in the introduction. The phrase to keep in mind is 'target-task-agnostic VLA-RL optimization in world models.' That is the real claim. Then read the method section with attention to the interfaces: how the imagined video is produced, how Qwen3-VL is prompted to score success, what is frozen versus trainable, and how GRPO consumes the filtered rewards. The dual-noise verification mechanism deserves close reading because it addresses a failure mode that is easy to miss if one only looks at average success rates.

In the experiments, focus on the exact train-test split for LIBERO and the real-robot setup using only play data for world-model pretraining. Those details determine whether the results truly demonstrate transferable physical priors or just hidden task overlap. If time is limited, read the pipeline figure, the main LIBERO table, the ablation on reward verification, and the real-robot results. If reading deeply, ask two questions: is the main bottleneck coverage of interaction data or fidelity of the generative world model, and is VLM-based reward merely noisy or systematically biased on imagined videos. Expect roughly 45–70 minutes for a careful pass, longer if you also inspect appendices and failure cases.38:T43a,An end-to-end curation pipeline that composes multiple levers instead of isolating one:
- Joint multimodal deduplication: removes redundancy across image-text pairs (not just text-only or image-only), targeting higher effective sample diversity.
- Joint image-and-text filtering: quality filtering based on learnability and alignment (e.g., caption-image consistency, OCR readability, low-information captions, noisy/watermarked images).
- Mixture design via target-distribution matching: adjusts the training mix to better cover capability axes (OCR/docs, charts, math, counting, spatial/3D, branding, captioning, VQA, grounding, etc.), mitigating the “generic web caption” bias.
- Synthetic data: both task-agnostic and task-specific generation to fill long-tail phenomena and to shape supervision for harder axes.
- Strict controls: fixed backbone/vision encoder (Qwen3 LM + SigLIP2), fixed compute and recipe; baseline uses the same source dataset without curation. Both baseline and curated runs undergo the same multimodal decontamination to reduce test leakage confounds.39:T56c,The analysis starts from GRPO. For each prompt, the policy samples a group of responses, receives response-level rewards, normalizes advantages within the group, and optimizes a clipped policy objective. At a decoding state s_t, the next-token distribution is represented by softmax logits. For a sampled token y_t with advantage A, the update from A log π(y_t|s_t) increases the sampled token logit and redistributes probability mass according to the current distribution. The paper derives a first-order approximation of the local entropy change ΔH_t after this logit perturbation. The decomposition contains a sampled-token term and a state-wise distributional correction. Entropy polarity is the resulting signed quantity that predicts whether the update expands or contracts entropy and by how much. The asymmetry is important: reinforcing an already high-probability continuation tends to make the distribution sharper, while entropy expansion usually requires a lower-probability sample with positive advantage or a strong correction from the surrounding distribution. Papo then uses polarity as an online control signal. It reweights token-level advantages differently for positive- and negative-polarity updates, increasing entropy-expanding pressure when collapse is likely and allowing entropy-contracting updates when the policy needs to consolidate reward-aligned behavior.3a:T8f7,方法叫 ActGuide-RL。核心思想不是把 action data 当监督标签直接 imitation，而是在 RL rollout 过程中把它作为“计划式参考引导”注入，帮助策略访问原本到不了的高价值状态。作者强调两件事：一是 guidance 只在必要时介入，二是 guided rollout 不能停留在外部辅助层面，而要被内部化回 unguided policy。

论文先给出 reachability dynamics 的形式化。策略在某个状态之后还能成功的上界记为 Ψ(s)，再定义 effective state-visiting mass 来衡量 rollout 过程中还剩多少“成功潜力”。当策略不断进入低 Ψ 的状态，质量会乘法式衰减；一旦 reward state 落在 out-region，普通 group RL 就拿不到正负对比，优势估计塌缩。这一套形式化虽然不是完整可计算理论，但足够解释为什么 zero RL 在难任务上会失效。

在此基础上，ActGuide-RL 引入 action guidance。给定任务和可用的动作轨迹数据，系统构造 plan-style reference guidance，在 rollout 时为模型提供一个可跟随但不强制照抄的行动参考。与直接 SFT 或 behavior cloning 的区别在于：这里 guidance 是在线探索辅助，而不是离线把策略拟合到示范分布。与给 CoT 的区别在于：作者刻意避免依赖后验合成 reasoning，因为那容易出现 rationalization，且成本高。

作者进一步分析了 guidance 的 benefit-risk trade-off。指导越强，越容易跨过 reachability barrier；但越强也越偏离当前策略分布，带来 off-policy shift，最终可能学不到真正可自主执行的策略。基于这个观察，方法上提出两条原则。C1 是 minimal intervention：把 guidance 作为 zero-reward fallback，仅在模型自己走不通时启用，并尽量自适应减少介入。C2 是 mixed-policy optimization：把 guided 和 unguided rollouts 一起用于训练，让模型把通过引导获得的探索收益内化回自身策略，而不是长期依赖外部提示。

从方法定位看，它介于三类方案之间：不是纯 RL，因为用了外部动作参考；不是纯 imitation，因为优化目标仍是环境 reward；也不是标准 SFT+RL 两阶段，因为没有先做冷启动监督拟合。这是它最有意思的地方。3b:T661,The method, ActGuide-RL, uses action data as plan-style reference guidance during RL rollouts. The goal is not to imitate demonstrations offline, but to help the policy cross reachability barriers online so that reward-bearing states become visitable.

The paper first formalizes the problem through reachability dynamics. Let Ψ(s) denote the supremum success probability achievable from state s under any continuation policy. Based on this, the authors define an effective state-visiting mass that tracks how much remaining success potential is preserved along rollouts. When trajectories drift into low-Ψ regions, this mass decays multiplicatively. In hard tasks, the base policy may never reach states from which success is possible, which explains why zero-shot RL produces no learning signal.

ActGuide-RL then injects action guidance as a reference plan. The model can use this guidance to navigate toward promising states, but the guidance is not meant to become a permanent crutch. The paper emphasizes a benefit-risk trade-off: stronger guidance improves exploration, but it also increases off-policy distribution shift and may prevent the policy from internalizing the behavior.

This leads to two design principles. First, minimal intervention: guidance should act as a zero-reward fallback and be used adaptively only when necessary. Second, mixed-policy optimization: guided and unguided rollouts are jointly optimized so that the gains from guided exploration are absorbed into the unguided policy itself. This makes the method distinct from pure RL, pure behavior cloning, and the standard two-stage SFT+RL pipeline.3c:T4f7,Experiments are conducted on search-agent benchmarks with different base models, difficulty levels, and both in-domain and out-of-domain settings. The main result is consistent improvement over zero RL, with larger gains on harder tasks where unguided RL fails to discover reward states.

For Qwen3-4B-Instruct, the reported gains over zero RL are +10.68 points on GAIA, +27.79 on WebWalkerQA, +19.00 on XBench, and +5.15 on BC-ZH. These are large enough to suggest that the method is solving a reachability bottleneck rather than merely polishing already-solvable tasks.

Another important result is that ActGuide-RL performs on par with the SFT+RL pipeline even without any cold-start initialization. That is the strongest practical claim in the paper, because reducing dependence on warm-start supervision is the whole point of the method.

The most important ablations concern guidance strength and policy mixing. The paper argues that more guidance is not always better; the best regime is to use guidance as a minimal fallback. A second key question is whether mixed-policy training truly improves the unguided policy, rather than only making the guided policy look better. Any separate reporting of guided versus unguided evaluation is especially informative here.3d:T460,This is a good skim paper. Start with the introduction and the motivating figure to understand the in-region versus out-region distinction. Then read the reachability-dynamics section just deeply enough to grasp why zero-reward trajectories produce no useful gradient under group-based RL. After that, focus on the actual mechanics of action guidance, the minimal-intervention rule, and mixed-policy optimization. Finish with the main result tables and the ablations on guidance strength.

Three questions are worth keeping in mind. First, is this genuinely better than repackaged imitation learning, or does it preserve the exploration and credit-assignment advantages of RL? Second, how sensitive is it to the quality and coverage of the action data? Third, if the base model becomes much stronger, does the gain shrink quickly, implying that this is mainly a cold-start remedy rather than a general training paradigm?

A solid first pass should take about 25 to 35 minutes. If you work on agentic post-training, the appendix is worth checking for rollout mixing details, fallback triggers, and out-of-domain behavior.3e:T603,The proposed method, dGRPO, combines Group Relative Policy Optimization with on-policy distillation. The key idea is not merely adding two losses together, but aligning them on the same on-policy trajectories. First, the student samples its own long-context rollouts, so training happens on the model-induced state distribution rather than on fixed expert traces. Second, each rollout receives two learning signals: a GRPO-style relative reward signal for direct task optimization, and a token-level teacher distillation signal that provides dense next-token guidance under the same prefixes. Third, the implementation removes the usual reference-policy KL term by setting beta_ref to zero, following recent reasoning-model practice, which reduces memory/compute overhead and allows the policy to move farther from initialization when that helps reasoning.

The paper positions the method against three alternatives. SFT/KD gives dense supervision but is off-policy and does not optimize arbitrary rewards. GRPO is on-policy and reward-driven but sparse. On-policy distillation is on-policy and dense but not reward-optimizing. dGRPO is meant to inherit all three desirable properties at once: on-policy sampling, dense token guidance, and direct reward optimization. The accompanying LONG-BLOCKS synthetic dataset is also part of the recipe. It covers multi-hop reasoning, contextual grounding, and long-form generation, so the method is evaluated and trained on a broader long-context task mix rather than a narrow retrieval-style setup.3f:T45e,The experimental contribution is mainly comparative and recipe-oriented. The paper contrasts SFT/KD, OPD, GRPO, and dGRPO in a Qwen3-based setup for long-context reasoning. The qualitative conclusion is consistent throughout the text: off-policy methods help with structure and imitation but struggle to recover from self-generated errors over long horizons; pure GRPO can improve reward alignment but is less stable and less sample-efficient; dGRPO improves long-context performance more consistently while better preserving the base model’s short-context capability.

However, the provided full-text excerpt does not include the complete result tables, appendix numbers, model sizes, token budgets, or exact benchmark gains. So it would be unsafe to invent precise figures. What should be checked in the full paper is clear: performance as a function of context length, stability metrics during training, and retention of short-context general abilities after long-context alignment. Those three axes determine whether the gains come from a genuinely better training signal or simply from task/data specialization.40:T441,ATESD builds on OPSD. In OPSD, the student conditions only on the problem and samples an on-policy rollout. The teacher is the same model under a privileged context that includes the reference solution, and it provides token-level distributions along the student rollout. Training minimizes a clipped per-token forward KL, with gradients flowing only through the student. ATESD changes the teacher context from full reference exposure to a continuous exposure level α∈[0,1]. A preliminary fixed-exposure sweep shows that intermediate exposure, such as α=0.5, can outperform full exposure, while teacher-student mismatch grows monotonically with α. The method uses a lightweight Beta-policy controller that maps compact training-state statistics to an exposure value. The controller holds one global exposure over a window, while the student updates every distillation step. The controller is trained with REINFORCE using a discounted learning-progress reward, so exposure decisions are credited by their effect on future student improvement rather than by immediate KD-loss reduction.41:T408,GEAR reshapes the trajectory-level GRPO advantage using local weights derived from self-distillation. It constructs a task-grounded teacher by conditioning the same model on ground-truth information, and compares this teacher distribution with the on-policy student distribution. The reverse KL between the two distributions is used as a reference-guided deviation signal. Large reverse KL values indicate positions where the student starts to depart from a task-correct continuation. The method then uses token entropy as a boundary signal, since entropy spikes often mark uncertainty or semantic transitions, and expands pointwise divergence into adaptive behavioral segments. The resulting token-level aligned regions and segment-level divergent regions are converted into local credit weights. The reweighting depends on the sign of the trajectory advantage: in successful trajectories, highly divergent regions are down-weighted to avoid reinforcing unstable deviations; in failed trajectories, they receive stronger penalties.42:T653,The method converts chat-style interaction into a table of parallel token streams, where each column corresponds to a role or channel such as user, system, assistant, thinking, or an auxiliary monitoring stream. At each step, the model attends to the history of all streams and predicts the next token for multiple output streams in one forward pass. Instead of concatenating all roles into one sequence with delimiter tokens, the model performs joint conditional generation over structured streams.

The important detail is that this is not just batched decoding of several independent sequences. The streams are coupled through interdependent attention. The paper frames the change as an instruction-tuning modification: existing message-based data can be transformed into aligned multi-stream supervision, and additional stream annotations can be synthesized from existing chat models. Empty cells are explicitly modeled and can be skipped at inference time to reduce KV-cache growth. The setup also distinguishes input streams, which can receive live external tokens, from output streams, which are generated by the model. That is what enables overlapping reading, thinking, and acting.

Compared with standard chat tuning, the structural information is no longer encoded only through role markers and sequence order. It is built into the tensor layout and attention pattern. The paper also argues that auxiliary internal streams are useful because they do not need to serve as user-facing explanations. They can therefore expose intent or situational awareness more directly than a public chain-of-thought channel.43:T50c,The empirical focus is on system behavior rather than perplexity. The paper reports large reductions in time-to-first-token and lower end-to-end latency by overlapping reading, reasoning, and acting. It also claims that throughput remains close to standard decoding because inference is often memory-bound, so predicting multiple streams in one pass does not linearly multiply latency. Task performance is said to remain largely preserved despite the format change.

Security experiments center on prompt-injection robustness. By explicitly separating system, user, and self-generated content into different streams, the model is better able to track provenance, which improves robustness in long and mixed-source contexts. Monitorability experiments examine whether extra internal streams reveal situational awareness or intent that does not appear in visible output or the main reasoning stream.

From a pretraining researcher’s perspective, the key open questions are whether the gains hold across scale, how much the training data construction depends on teacher-generated supervision, whether the serving-side cache and scheduling advantages survive real deployment constraints, and whether benchmark improvements come from the multi-stream structure itself or from richer supervision.44:T41c,First maps the full OP(S)D design space across three dimensions: teacher construction (self-distillation / frozen teacher / EMA teacher), privileged information (PI) types (none / answer only / full response / system prompt), and distillation loss design (full-vocab KL / sampled-token policy gradient / Top-K reverse KL / stop-gradient Top-K). Then identifies three core failure mechanisms: 1) student-generated prefixes push the teacher into local states where its token-level supervision is incompatible with the student trajectory; 2) Top-K reverse KL approximation introduces biased gradients that destabilize optimization; 3) OPSD only learns a single PI-free consensus policy across instances, which fails when PI is instance-specific (e.g., ground-truth answers for math problems). Finally proposes three targeted stabilizers: stop-gradient Top-K KL surrogate to eliminate biased gradients, RL with Verifiable Rewards (RLVR) adapted teachers to improve task alignment, and student SFT to standardize output format and stabilize response length.45:T40d,The paper separates the total importance ratio into two factors. The first factor repairs training-inference discrepancy by comparing the training-side and inference-side distributions at the same old policy version. The second factor corrects policy staleness by comparing the current training policy with the old training-side policy. These two factors should be controlled differently: discrepancy repair is naturally handled through masking, filtering, or down-weighting inconsistent tokens, while PPO clipping should apply to the staleness ratio with its advantage-sign-dependent boundary. The missing-old-logit problem removes the denominator needed for the staleness ratio. The paper also analyzes interpolation-based proxy references and argues that, under common constructions, they mostly reparameterize the effective clipping interval rather than recover the missing old policy. Practical repair options include exact old-logit acquisition through system support and a lower-cost PPO-EWMA reference policy as an approximation.46:T93b,The work has two layers: explanation and exploitation. On the explanation side, the authors start from a shared base model and compare RL and OPD through their parameter deltas, ΔW. They then run several analyses. First is norm-controlled scaling: keep the final update direction fixed and scale only its magnitude by a factor α, then evaluate models of the form W_base + αΔW. OPD delivers larger reasoning gains than RL at the same update norm, which implies that RL contains more task-weak components in its update. Second is module-level marginal utility analysis: they inspect sensitivity and accumulated update norm across layers and modules, asking where the model changes a lot without corresponding performance benefit. Their finding is not that RL and OPD care about completely different modules, but that RL spends much more update budget on low-utility regions, whereas OPD suppresses those regions early and concentrates on more reasoning-critical modules, especially in middle layers. They call this Functional Redundancy Avoidance. Third is spectral and subspace evolution analysis: they study singular value concentration of updates across checkpoints and measure alignment between early dominant subspaces and the final update subspace. OPD shows stronger low-rank concentration, earlier stabilization, and much earlier alignment with the final solution. RL remains more diffuse and keeps rotating its update directions later into training. Fourth is early-checkpoint recoverability: after module-wise norm scaling, an OPD checkpoint at roughly 10% of training recovers about 80% of the final reasoning performance. This is a strong empirical sign that the main structure of the final solution is already present very early.

On the exploitation side, they propose EffOPD. The idea is simple: if OPD directions are already close to the final direction early in training, then one can linearly extrapolate along the current update direction and reduce the number of optimization steps and samples. This is not a new architecture or loss; it is a plug-and-play acceleration strategy built on the observed trajectory stability. The important point is not linear extrapolation by itself, but the condition under which it works: the training path must be directionally stable enough. The earlier spectral analysis is used to justify that condition.47:T61b,The experiments span models from 1.5B to 32B parameters, including pretrained, SFT-tuned, and thinking-style models. RL baselines include PPO, GRPO, and DAPO. OPD uses pattern-aligned teachers, typically stronger models from the same family. The main empirical results are fourfold. First, under fixed update direction and matched update norm, OPD yields substantially larger reasoning gains than RL, supporting the claim that OPD updates are more task-relevant rather than merely smaller or larger. Second, module-level analysis shows that RL accumulates more redundant updates in low-marginal-utility modules, while OPD concentrates more on modules that matter for reasoning. Third, spectral analysis shows stronger low-rank concentration and earlier final-subspace alignment for OPD; RL updates are more dispersed and continue to drift later in training. Fourth, EffOPD delivers about 3× average training acceleration across multiple settings from 1.5B to 32B while maintaining final performance comparable to vanilla OPD. One especially important number appears early in the paper: an OPD checkpoint at only around 10% of training can recover roughly 80% of final reasoning performance after module-wise norm scaling. That is the clearest quantitative evidence for early directional lock-in.

When reading the full paper, it is worth checking how robust these findings are across tasks, teacher-student gaps, and RL variants. That determines whether “foresight” is a broad property of OPD or mainly a feature of same-family, pattern-aligned distillation.48:T4d3,Read this one by following the claims, not the paper order. Start with the norm-controlled scaling result around Figure 2. That establishes the key point: OPD is not just moving parameters differently in size; it is using the same update budget more effectively. Then read the Functional Redundancy Avoidance section and inspect whether sensitivity and update norm really decouple at the module level, and which modules are identified as low utility. Next, read the spectral and subspace evolution analysis. Focus on two questions: how early does low-rank concentration emerge, and how exactly is alignment with the final solution measured? Finish with EffOPD and judge whether it is a robust engineering trick or something that depends strongly on a particular trajectory shape.

If time is limited to 20 minutes, read the introduction, the main scaling figure, the spectral plots, and the main EffOPD table. If you have an hour, the appendix is worth reading for consistency across scales and RL baselines. For a pretraining audience, the most interesting follow-up question is not OPD itself, but whether early dominant-subspace lock-in also appears in large-scale pretraining, mid-training domain shifts, or SFT warm-start regimes.49:T558,The proposed framework is Token-level Bregman Preference Optimization, or TBPO. The starting point is a token-level Bradley–Terry preference model defined over next-token actions conditioned on a prefix state. Instead of treating a completion as an indivisible object, the method assigns a token-level score to each state-action pair and learns the corresponding preference density ratio. The estimation objective is built from a Bregman divergence family, which makes standard logistic-style DPO only one special case rather than the default formulation.

The paper introduces two concrete parameterizations. TBPO-Q defines the token-level score through a state-action value function Q. TBPO-A defines it through an advantage function A, emphasizing relative improvement over the state baseline. The derivation is grounded in a KL-regularized sequential decision formulation of autoregressive generation. Using closed-form expressions for optimal Q or A under a reference policy, the authors derive a token-level ratio-matching loss that can be trained from ordinary sequence-level preference pairs. The claimed theoretical payoff is stronger than prior token-level DPO variants: under the token-level preference model, minimizing the TBPO objective recovers the desired token-wise optimal policy, rather than merely improving a decomposed sequence-level objective.4a:T40f,The paper claims three empirical benefits: better alignment than strong baselines across benchmarks and backbones, improved training stability, and better preservation of response diversity. The excerpt provided here does not include the full result tables, exact model sizes, dataset sizes, or numerical gains, so precise figures should not be fabricated. What is clear from the text is that the empirical comparison is centered on DPO-style baselines and token-level extensions such as TDPO and TISDPO, not on a large-scale RLHF comparison.

The most informative experiments to inspect in the full paper are likely these: TBPO-Q versus TBPO-A, to understand whether value-based or advantage-based scoring is more robust; ablations over different Bregman generators, to test whether the generalized objective family matters in practice; and long-generation settings, where token-level credit assignment should matter most. Diversity metrics are also important because the paper explicitly claims better diversity retention than baselines.4b:T428,TextSeal keeps the distortion-free property of Gumbel-max sampling: the marginal next-token distribution is unchanged under the intended decoding setup. It adds three components. First, dual-key generation randomly routes each token through one of two secret keys with routing probability α, restoring output diversity while retaining a detectable signal. The detector uses early fusion, aggregating scores under both keys instead of testing each key separately and combining p-values later. Second, entropy-weighted detection gives more weight to positions where the model distribution has higher entropy, since those tokens carry more watermark information. Entropy is estimated with a proxy model, and moment-matched Gamma approximations are used for calibrated p-values. Third, localized detection searches multiple regions in a document to find watermarked spans, which improves detection under dilution. The design also supports speculative decoding and multi-token prediction by assigning keys to draft and fallback paths without adding generation latency.4c:T65c,The paper builds a systematic binary-classification setup around Qwen3 8B, 32B, and 235B. Training uses LoRA rather than full fine-tuning, with rank 32 and alpha 32 adapters applied to all attention and MLP projection layers. The supervised objective is masked SFT: all tokens except the final classification token are masked out of the loss, so the optimization signal is concentrated on the classification decision itself. Evaluation is done in two modes. In 1-token classification, the model outputs 0 or 1, and the score is computed from the normalized probability of 1. In thinking classification, the model reasons first and then outputs a confidence score from 0 to 100. The datasets include Chemical harm (50k), Cyber harm (50k), ControlArena (4k), IMDB (25k), and Covert Reasoning (1.5k). Two design choices are especially important. First, the authors separate adjacent-domain transfer from same-domain, new-prompt transfer; the former tests semantic transfer, while the latter tests whether the model is actually following the prompt. Second, they compare ways of combining classification data with general instruction-following data. Simple mixing works poorly because classification examples contribute very few unmasked tokens, so their training signal is diluted by ordinary instruction data. A better strategy is stagewise training: do classification tuning first, then instruction tuning. This preserves more of the classification gain while reducing prompt-following failures. The result is a useful reminder that data mixture should be analyzed in terms of effective loss contribution, not just sample counts.4d:T5e6,There are four main empirical takeaways. First, transfer across adjacent domains is real. A representative result is that training on 'is this cyber harm?' recovers about 75% of the improvement that would be obtained by directly training on the target task 'does the AI do anything dangerous?'. Second, when in-domain data is scarce, adding data from an adjacent domain still helps, even under strong dataset imbalance. Third, there are clear generalization failures: after single-task training, the model can apply the old classification rule to similar transcripts even when the prompt has changed. In other words, it sometimes behaves like a domain-conditioned labeler rather than a prompt-conditioned classifier. Fourth, adding instruction-following training mitigates this, but naive mixing is worse than stagewise training because the classification objective is underweighted in token-level loss. The generative-transfer results are also notable: for Qwen3-8B, training only on single-token cyber-harm classification raises thinking-classification AUC from 0.84 to 0.94. The same single-token training also improves dangerous-action detection when the model is used as a summarizer. The paper further explores an unsupervised elicitation loop where predictions from thinking classification are used to generate new single-token training targets. Metrics are mainly AUROC, with logAUC used for stronger classifiers to emphasize the low-FPR regime from 10^-3 to 10^-1, which is closer to deployment needs.4e:T440,For a quick but useful read, start with the experimental setup: understand the difference between 1-token and thinking classification, the masked-loss training scheme, and the relationships among the datasets. Then focus on Section 4 for transfer results and Section 4.6 for generalization failures; these sections show most clearly what classification tuning improves and what it breaks. If you mainly care about engineering implications, read the experiments on instruction-data mixing and stage ordering next; about 30 minutes should be enough to extract the main lessons. Three details deserve closer attention. First, why classification samples contribute too few effective training tokens, making naive mixing misleadingly weak. Second, why single-token training transfers to reasoning-style classification, which hints at changes beyond output formatting. Third, the same-domain, new-prompt failure cases, which matter directly for monitors, reward models, and policy classifiers because real deployments often change task definitions without changing the surface form of the data.4f:T503,The framework is called Detect-Classify-Compare. It first segments a generated CoT into reasoning steps using a rule-based parser over newlines, transition markers, and sentence boundaries; the reported parsing F1 is 0.87. At each step boundary, it estimates internal answer commitment with a logit-lens proxy. Hidden states from each layer are projected through the unembedding and layer normalization, and the probability assigned to the ground-truth answer token, p_ans, is tracked. A step is treated as internally committed when p_ans exceeds τ=0.3. The visible-trace label is constructed by checking whether the cumulative CoT text has already exposed the ground-truth answer string or a normalized variant. Belief-CoT Agreement measures whether these two binary states match over time. Mismatches are then classified into step-level patterns, with confabulated continuation being the dominant one: the answer proxy is already stable while the model keeps writing deliberative-looking text. The paper also compares architecture-matched Qwen2.5 and DeepSeek-R1-Distill model pairs to isolate the effect of reasoning distillation. To reduce dependence on a single probe, the logit-lens signal is cross-validated with Patchscopes, tuned-lens probes, and causal direction ablation.50:T46a,The study covers nine models and seven reasoning benchmarks. The main quantitative result is that latent commitment and explicit answer arrival align on only 61.9% of steps on average. Among detected mismatch events, 58.0% are confabulated continuation. The validation checks are important: Patchscopes agrees with the logit-lens readout on 75.6% of mid-depth steps and 74.7% of final-layer steps; logit-lens-only disagreement is near zero, at 0.0% for mid-depth and 1.5% for final-layer steps; tuned-lens binary classifications agree with the logit lens at at least 99.2% across four held-out model-benchmark configurations. In controlled Qwen2.5 versus DeepSeek-R1-Distill comparisons, reasoning training changes both aggregate alignment and the mix of failure modes. The 32B pair is especially informative: confabulated steps decrease while contradictory states increase. The paper also links timing faithfulness to behavior: lower step-level alignment tends to come with larger CoT utility, and paired truncation plus donor-corruption experiments suggest that much post-commitment text is descriptive rather than load-bearing.51:T5ef,DiffScore reframes evaluation as masked reconstruction. A candidate text is corrupted under varying masking rates, and a bidirectional model is asked to recover masked tokens; higher-quality text should be more confidently recoverable under diverse masking patterns.
- Backbone: Masked Large Diffusion Language Models (MDLLMs) with an absorbing mask token [M]. The forward process masks each token independently with probability t; the reverse model is a non-causal (bidirectional) Transformer predicting clean tokens at masked positions.
- DiffScore-Zero: zero-shot scoring using the MDLLM ELBO. A key detail is the 1/t reweighting term, compensating for the expected number of masked tokens (~tL) so that contributions across timesteps are comparable.
- DiffScore-FT: task-adaptive fine-tuning without gold references, intended to isolate the gain from the evaluation paradigm rather than reference-based supervision.
- Diagnostics: (1) multi-timestep quality profiles that decompose the score across masking rates (low t ≈ local fluency; high t ≈ global coherence), and (2) a bidirectional PMI-style decomposition to separate fluency vs faithfulness/coverage dimensions—argued to be structurally difficult for AR scoring due to directional coupling.
Core difference from BARTScore/GPTScore is not merely swapping models, but changing the scoring factorization: from a single left-to-right likelihood to a recoverability-based score aggregated over masking severities, reducing positional/directional artifacts.52:T86d,方法核心是 RuDE（Rubric-based Discriminative Evaluation）。它不要求 base model 直接生成高质量回答，而是让模型在“好回答 / 坏回答”的对比中做判别，用判别能力去代理未来的生成潜力。这个设计建立在文中引用的 generation-evaluation consistency 假设上：一个模型若能稳定分辨更符合任务要求的回答，通常也更有可能在 post-training 后学会生成这类回答。

为了解决开放式任务评估标准不统一的问题，作者提出 4C taxonomy，把回答质量拆成四类约束：Competence、Content、Control、Compliance。虽然全文片段里没有把每一类展开到最细，但从任务构造可以看出，它们分别覆盖能力完成度、内容正确与充分性、格式/风格/结构控制，以及对指令和规则的遵守。作者不是直接写一个总分 rubric，而是为每个任务手工定义细粒度 rubric，再用这些 rubric 组合出 preferred / dispreferred response pairs。

数据构造上，作者把四类开放任务改写成 response discrimination 形式，覆盖 Medical、Legal & Finance、Complex Instruction Following、Creative Writing。关键是 generator-verifier pipeline：先生成候选回答，再通过 rubric 控制，构造“满足全部 rubric 的 preferred response”和“故意违反部分 rubric 的 dispreferred response”。这样得到的不是粗糙的正负样本，而是带有可解释错误类型的对比样本。RuDE 评估时，base model 只需判断哪一个回答更好，或者对候选回答做相对偏好打分。

和已有代理指标相比，区别很明确。MMLU 测的是知识回忆和选择题判断，PPL 测的是 token-level 拟合；两者都不直接触及开放式对齐任务中的格式控制、意图理解、约束满足。RuDE 则把评估对象从“知识是否记住”转成“是否具备学会对齐行为的判别基础”。这也是它与传统 reward model eval、preference eval 的不同之处：这里评的是未经 post-train 的 base model，并且目标是预测其可塑性，而不是评估一个已对齐模型的当前偏好质量。53:T733,The core method is RuDE, short for Rubric-based Discriminative Evaluation. Instead of asking a base model to generate a strong open-ended answer, RuDE asks it to discriminate between a preferred and a dispreferred response. The motivation is that discrimination is easier than generation for a base model, and that the ability to recognize better responses may be a good proxy for the ability to learn to generate them after post-training.

To make open-ended evaluation systematic, the paper introduces a 4C taxonomy: Competence, Content, Control, and Compliance. These dimensions cover whether the response completes the task, whether the content is correct and adequate, whether formatting/style/structure constraints are followed, and whether explicit instructions or rules are obeyed. For each task, the authors manually define fine-grained rubrics under these dimensions.

The data pipeline is also important. Four open-ended domains are converted into response-discrimination datasets: medical, legal and finance, complex instruction following, and creative writing. A generator-verifier pipeline constructs contrastive pairs. Preferred responses satisfy all rubrics, while dispreferred responses intentionally violate selected rubrics. This gives the benchmark more structure than generic preference pairs, because the negative examples are tied to interpretable failure modes.

Relative to prior proxies, the distinction is clear. MMLU mainly measures knowledge recall and multiple-choice discrimination; perplexity measures token-level fit to pretraining data. Neither directly targets the ingredients that matter in post-training, such as instruction adherence, controllability, or alignment with human intent. RuDE instead evaluates whether the base model already contains a discriminative substrate that post-training can exploit.54:T522,The main empirical claim is strong correlation with downstream post-training outcomes: RuDE scores achieve Pearson correlation above 90% with the performance of fully instruction-tuned models. This is substantially stronger than the weak correlation reported for traditional base-model metrics such as MMLU on open-ended post-training tasks.

The evaluation spans four domains and multiple pretrained base models, followed by actual post-training through instruction tuning or RL. The most concrete case highlighted in the paper is that RuDE predicts Qwen3-4B-Base to have higher post-training potential than Qwen2.5-7B-Base, and this ranking is later validated after RL. That result matters because it shows RuDE is not merely a size proxy.

The likely ablation message is threefold. First, discriminative evaluation is more reliable than direct generative evaluation for base models because it avoids the instruction-following bottleneck. Second, fine-grained rubric-controlled contrastive pairs are more informative than coarse preference labels. Third, evaluating across diverse open-ended domains improves robustness of the ranking signal. The excerpt does not include all tables, but the central result is clear: the ranking induced by RuDE tracks post-training outcomes under both instruction tuning and RL.55:T44e,For a skim-level read, focus on the introduction, task formulation, the 4C taxonomy, and the main correlation tables. Figure 1 is the anchor: it shows why MMLU-like metrics are insufficient for this problem. Then read the formal ranking setup to understand what exactly is being predicted. After that, inspect the generator-verifier pipeline and a few rubric examples to see whether the benchmark construction is careful or superficial.

Three questions are worth keeping in mind while reading. First, is RuDE measuring genuine post-training plasticity, or mostly latent knowledge plus local preference discrimination? Second, how well do the 4C dimensions cover reasoning-heavy or tool-using post-training objectives? Third, if used for checkpoint selection, could it favor models that are good judges but not necessarily the easiest to optimize into strong generators?

A first pass should take around 30 to 45 minutes. If you want to operationalize it, spend extra time on the appendix for rubric design, pair construction quality control, and the statistical stability of the reported correlations.56:T4a3,They evaluate LLM-based monitors that take full agent transcripts (user/assistant/tool calls/results) and output a danger label. The main experimental knob is adding massive benign prefixes (up to ~800K tokens) so the malicious action occurs deep inside the context, then measuring recall drop.

Models: Claude Opus 4.6, Gemini 3.1 Pro, GPT 5.4 Thinking, with explicit sweeps over “thinking” modes/budgets and prompt variants (standard CoT, a ‘list suspicious actions verbatim first’ variant, and GPT-specific prompt adjustments to control false positives).

Mitigations are tested at three levels:
1) Inference/prompting: stronger reasoning instructions, more thinking tokens, structured CoT that forces evidence listing.
2) Input interventions: periodic reminders inserted throughout the transcript to keep the classifier anchored to the monitoring task.
3) System strategy: incremental monitoring over growing sub-transcripts (optionally subsampled for cost) instead of a single end-of-session call.

They also try a training mitigation: supervised fine-tuning GPT-4.1 on long-context classification data, and check whether improvements generalize beyond near-identical tasks.57:T4e9,The approach is a three-way alignment between (i) corpus exposure, (ii) an external popularity proxy, and (iii) model judgments.
1) Full-corpus exposure measurement: the authors index the complete OLMo training corpora (pretraining, midtraining, long-context) with Infini-Gram. The combined data is ~27TB / 7.4T tokens; indexing is sharded (128 shards) and yields ~50TB of indexed artifacts, enabling exact entity/alias counting at scale.
2) Entity set construction: entities are sampled from Wikidata across five types (Person, Location, Organization, Art, Product). Aliases are used to improve recall, but alias ambiguity is treated as a key confounder, motivating validation/cleaning steps.
3) External popularity signal: Wikipedia pageviews are used as a real-world popularity proxy.
4) Model elicitation: two protocols on OLMo-3-7B and OLMo-3.1-32B—Direct (ask for popularity) and Pairwise Comparison (which entity is more popular). The paper treats pairwise comparison as a more stable behavioral readout, less sensitive to calibration and free-form generation artifacts.
5) Analysis strategy: correlations are examined not only globally but also by entity type and popularity quantiles to surface head vs tail differences and type-specific effects.58:T47f,The work instantiates the Bayesian ‘hypothesis space’ as a metric conceptual space and lifts it to a belief space of distributions.
1) Conceptual space: following Gärdenfors, concepts live in a low-dimensional attribute space with a distance metric encoding similarity; concepts correspond to regions.
2) Conceptual belief space: beliefs are probability distributions over the conceptual space; belief updating becomes motion (a trajectory) in this lifted space.
3) Setting: story understanding is used as a natural sequential evidence-accumulation environment, where new narrative information forces continual belief revision.
4) Evidence stack:
- Behavioral: model outputs over story progression reflect systematic belief changes.
- Representational: linear probes on hidden states predict positions/updates in the proposed space.
- Interventional: manipulating hidden representations can steer the trajectory and alter subsequent ICL behavior.
A key design choice is to avoid fully specifying the Bayesian update rule for arbitrary text; instead, the paper tests whether a stable low-dimensional geometry exists that can support such dynamics.59:T407,A two-stage associative memory architecture is proposed under the modular Hopfield energy framework: 1) A context gate layer is added, with one gate neuron per stored memory. Context activates gates via a bilinear energy term, and a positive-energy self-connection term based on inter-memory similarity inherently induces sparsity in gate activations (gates for similar memories suppress each other). 2) The gate layer and retrieval layer are connected via a coupling energy term, and the retrieval layer uses a LogSumExp Lagrangian to output a distribution over memories. 3) Theoretical proofs show context gating increases effective inter-memory separation, leading to exponential retrieval performance gains, and the coupled system has a unique self-consistent fixed point, meaning retrieval results are driven by both direct contextual bias and non-linear gate-retrieval feedback. 4) A first-order approximation of the model is mapped to internal Transformer mechanisms to verify that native LLM dynamics align with the theory.5a:T45b,A two-stage training paradigm is adopted: The first stage is a scalable interleaved GUI-tool data construction pipeline. Based on existing static GUI corpora, MLLMs are used to synthesize trajectory-aware tool libraries from recurring GUI operation procedures. Pure GUI data is converted to interleaved GUI-tool trajectories through tool step generation and next-state grounding, eliminating the need to collect high-cost real tool trajectories. The second stage is hierarchical reinforcement fine-tuning: first, warmup supervised fine-tuning is used to obtain basic hybrid action capabilities, then single-turn RL is applied to optimize decisions at explicit GUI-tool switching points, finally online agentic RL is performed in a realistic GUI-tool environment. The reward function includes a tool appropriateness term (incentivizing tool calls when beneficial and abstention when unnecessary) and a path efficiency term (encouraging replacement of redundant GUI operations with tool calls to shorten trajectories), providing trajectory-level feedback to guide the model to select globally optimal execution paths.5b:T444,The interaction is formalized as a discrete-time stochastic process. User actions are drawn from true user dynamics P(u_t | h_{t-1}), while agent actions follow a policy π(a_t | h_{t-1}, u_t). The desired simulator should generate from the real user distribution conditioned on a control variable and should remain valid when interacting with a target policy different from the logging policy. The key technical point is that post-hoc trajectory labels are not ordinary controls: they are functions of the full trajectory and are entangled with future actions of the behavior policy. This creates look-ahead bias. Using ideas from off-policy evaluation, the paper further shows that when the evaluation policy diverges from the behavior policy, counterfactual metric variance can grow geometrically, a failure mode named controllability collapse. The proposed mitigations are: use only a priori controls fixed before interaction; use step-wise dynamic controls that depend only on observable history so far; or condition the simulator directly on the target policy to reduce policy mismatch.5c:T575,MEME defines a 2D taxonomy: entity scope (single vs. multi-entity) × temporal dynamics (static vs. evolving), then instantiates six tasks that intentionally avoid already-covered easy variants:
- Exact Recall (single/static, verbatim fidelity)
- Aggregation (multi/static, cross-session coverage)
- Tracking (single/evolving, full revision history)
- Deletion (single/evolving, stop reporting removed facts)
- Cascade (multi/evolving, propagate updates through explicit dependency rules)
- Absence (multi/evolving, recognize uncertainty when upstream changes invalidate a dependent fact without a replacement rule)

Data is generated from a DAG knowledge graph with explicit conditional rules, enabling gold answers by construction. A key methodological step is an “in-context solvability” check: feeding gold facts directly to the answering LLM to confirm the tasks are solvable in principle, isolating failures to memory write/retrieve/update mechanisms.

The main diagnosis is retrieval-time failure: stale values outrank update events (notably in vector retrieval), or tool/graph/sparse retrieval fails to surface the right evidence, so the answering LLM defaults to the earlier value. The only strong closure reported comes from moving propagation to ingest-time: a strong internal LLM writes the propagated value into the store so retrieval becomes a direct lookup of the current value.5d:T5ec,The paper has three main components. First, an ecosystem audit: the authors inspect 50 popular training and evaluation repositories and check whether they preserve rollouts, expose failed/errored/skipped runs, and specify scoring rules. The central finding is simple and important: none of the audited repositories systematically report failed, errored, or skipped rollouts alongside headline scores. Second, a taxonomy of reporting-rule discrepancies: the paper catalogs 37 cases where differences in task-success definitions, cost/token accounting, or timing measurement can change the reported result even when the underlying evidence is fixed. The key point is not merely that conventions differ, but that these differences can materially alter conclusions. Third, the paper proposes rollout cards, a minimum-sufficient publication format in the lineage of datasheets and model cards. A rollout card includes task specification, agent actions, environment dynamics, terminal status, and the reporting rule behind each score, including which fields the rule reads and which it ignores. The authors also release a reference implementation and 21 card exports. The design choice is deliberate: instead of forcing a single evaluation stack, they require publication of re-analyzable evidence plus the rule used to score it. Compared with publishing only aggregate scores, this is closer to preserving trajectories in RL; compared with full environment replay, it is lighter-weight and focused on minimum sufficiency.5e:T47a,The experiments are organized into two settings. The first is re-analysis of partially released public artifacts, including GAP, MAESTRO, COPRA miniF2F selected logs, and Tree-of-Thought. These case studies show that rollout traces contain findings not reported in the original publications. For example, in GAP, 20.6% of agent responses certified as safe still made forbidden tool calls. In COPRA miniF2F logs, longer realized search correlates negatively, not positively, with proof success. The second setting holds benchmark artifacts fixed and changes only the reporting rule. Under this setup, reported scores can shift by as much as 20.9 percentage points. On τ-bench, different graders can reverse the ranking between GPT-4o and Claude 3.5 Sonnet. On MLE-Bench, pass rates change from 34.2% to 13.3% depending on medal/pass definitions. These results support the paper’s main claim: many apparent benchmark differences may reflect reporting choices rather than genuine capability differences. The experiments are not about improving model performance; they are about showing how much information is lost when only scores are published.15:["$","main",null,{"className":"flex-1 overflow-y-auto","children":["$","div",null,{"className":"mx-auto max-w-4xl px-4 py-8 sm:px-6 xl:max-w-6xl","children":["$","$L16",null,{"data":{"date":"2026-05-14","generated_at":"2026-05-14T01:16:02.398178+00:00","stats":{"total":158,"starred":10,"must_read":20,"skim":104,"glance":34,"by_category":{"pretraining_data":1,"industry":2,"llm_pretrain":16,"efficient":20,"multimodal":15,"multimodal_gen":12,"post_train":42,"mechanism":25,"coding_agent":10,"agent":15},"fetch_status":{}},"categories":[{"id":"pretraining_data","emoji":"📚","name":"预训练数据","description":"以预训练数据为核心贡献的工作：数据采集/清洗/去重、数据配比(data mixture)、数据筛选/质量评估、语料构建、数据课程(data curriculum)、数据重复(repetition)、合成数据用于预训练、Tokenizer/词表设计、成员推断/数据检测。判据是『论文核心是数据本身』，而非训练 recipe/优化器(→ llm_pretrain)或评测资产(→ eval)","papers":[{"id":"2605.11577","title":"BitLM: Unlocking Multi-Token Language Generation with Bitwise Continuous Diffusion","abstract":"Autoregressive language models generate text one token at a time, yet natural language is inherently structured in multi-token units, including phrases, n-grams, and collocations that carry meaning jointly. This simplex-based output geometry both constrains modeling and enforces inherently sequential decoding. The paper asks whether the vocabulary softmax should be the primary interface at all, and aims to enable native multi-token joint generation while preserving left-to-right causality (at least across blocks), rather than relying on post-hoc accelerators like speculative decoding.","importance":"The main contribution is a modeling-interface shift: replacing large-vocabulary classification with joint denoising over a small set of coupled binary variables. th","starred":true,"priority":"p1","reading_tier":"must_read","source":"arxiv","sources":["arxiv"],"published_at":"2026-05-12T17:51:26+00:00","category_id":"llm_pretrain","cover_url":"/covers/2605_12466.png","hf_upvotes":0,"arxiv_categories":["cs.LG","cs.AI","cs.CL","cs.NE"],"summary":{"core_problem":"这篇工作要解决的是“如何把 latent iterative refinement 真正做成可训练、可扩展、可部署的语言模型部件”。现有 looped / recurrent Transformer 路线有一个明确张力：它们希望让模型在输出 token 前先在隐空间里反复修正表示，从而获得更强的语言建模和推理能力；但一旦显式 unroll 多步，就会遇到训练不稳定、显存和反向成本随循环步数线性增长、推理串行开销大、循环深度难选且难泛化等问题。作者的目标不是再做一个“多循环几步”的变体，而是把循环 refinement 改写成固定点求解问题，用 equilibrium 视角替代显式展开。","importance":"这篇对预训练研究是实打实的重要工作，原因有三层。第一，它把 recurrent / latent reasoning 从一个常见但脆弱的实验方向，推进成了一个更像通用架构模块的东西：训练内存对 refinement 步数近似常数，步数由收敛自适应决定，而不是手工设定固定 loop depth。第二，它把 DEQ 式隐式层重新带回语言模型，但不是直接把整个 Transformer 做成平衡层，而是采用“backbone 先提议、attractor 再收敛”的两阶段结构，明显更贴近大规模 LM 的工程现实。第三，文中观察到的 equilibrium internalization 很关键：模型只用 next-token loss 训练，却会逐渐把原本需要迭代求解的 refinement 吸收到 backbone 初始表示里，导致后期求解步数下降。这对理解 latent computation、test-time compute、以及 pretrain 与推理时额外计算之间的关系都有直接启发。\n\n如果这个现象在更大规模上继续成立，它意味着“训练时允许慢思考，收敛后把一部分慢思考蒸馏回快路径”可能是一条值得认真研究的基座路线。","technical_approach":"$18","experiments":"实验覆盖两个 regime：大规模语言模型预训练，以及小模型高难推理。语言建模部分，作者将 Attractor Models 与标准 feed-forward Transformer、以及显式 unrolled 的 looped baselines 对比。结论是，在相近训练预算下，Attractor Model 能取得更好的语言建模表现，同时训练更稳，且不会像 looped baselines 那样因为循环步数增加而把显存和反向成本迅速推高。论文特别强调 constant-memory in effective depth：有效 refinement 深度增加时，训练内存基本不随之线性增长，这是方法成立的核心证据之一。\n\n在推理与小模型 reasoning 部分，Attractor Model 在 tiny-model hard reasoning 任务上优于已有递归模型，并且缓解了某些递归小模型里常见的“模型变大反而退化”的现象。作者把这点归因于固定点求解比显式展开更稳定，也更容易让模型学到可重复使用的 refinement dynamics。\n\n最值得注意的实验不是单个 benchmark 分数，而是训练动态分析。文中展示了收敛行为和 accuracy-vs-T 曲线：随着训练推进，backbone 初始预测与最终 fixed point 的距离缩小，达到近似平衡所需的迭代步数下降，而性能并未同步下降。这就是 equilibrium internalization 的直接证据。换句话说，模型在学会“把迭代过程内化”。\n\n如果精读全文，建议重点记录几类数字：1）不同模型规模下 attractor 模块相对 backbone 的大小；2）训练时平均求解步数与后期下降幅度；3）与 looped baselines 相比的显存、训练 FLOPs 或 wall-clock 差异；4）语言建模和 reasoning benchmark 上的具体提升。摘要里没有给出这些数字，但正文中的图表显然围绕这些量展开，它们决定这项方法到底是理论上优雅，还是工程上也站得住。","reading_guide":"这篇值得精读。第一遍先抓主线：Introduction 里对 looped LM 困境的界定很准确，Figure 1 和方法总览要看明白，尤其是“backbone proposal + attractor refinement”为什么比直接 recurrent LM 更稳。第二遍重点看 fixed-point formulation、solver、implicit differentiation 的推导和实现细节。这里决定了它是不是一个真正可扩展的训练方案，而不是把难题换个写法。\n\n实验部分建议按三条线读。第一条是语言建模主结果，关注与 feed-forward 和 looped baselines 的比较是否公平，训练预算是否对齐。第二条是 memory / compute / convergence 分析，这部分比 benchmark 分数更重要，因为它回答了 recurrent depth 能不能在大模型里用。第三条是 equilibrium internalization 相关图表，尤其是收敛步数随训练变化、初始 embedding 与 fixed point 距离变化、以及 accuracy-vs-iteration 的关系。这些图最有研究味道，值得反复看。\n\n如果你做 latent reasoning、test-time compute、DEQ、或 recurrent Transformer，这篇至少要花 60-90 分钟。先看方法和训练动态图，再回头核对附录里的求解器设置、稳定性技巧和消融。","authors_note":""},"summary_en":{"core_problem":"The paper addresses a central bottleneck in latent iterative computation for language models: how to make recurrent refinement stable, scalable, and practical. Existing looped or recurrent Transformers can improve language modeling and reasoning by refining hidden states before committing to a token, but explicit unrolling creates serious problems. Training becomes unstable, memory and backward cost grow linearly with the number of recurrent steps, inference is sequential and expensive, and the choice of recurrence depth is brittle. The paper replaces explicit unrolling with fixed-point solving, turning latent refinement into an equilibrium computation.","importance":"$19","technical_approach":"$1a","experiments":"$1b","reading_guide":"$1c","authors_note":""},"recommendation":{"zh":"将隐式微分和不动点求解引入 LLM 预训练，优雅地解决了循环 Transformer 的内存和稳定性问题，是探索非纯前馈架构（non-feed-forward）的重要尝试，必须精读其梯度计算和收敛性分析。","en":"Introducing implicit differentiation and fixed-point solving into LLM pretraining elegantly solves the memory and stability issues of looped Transformers; a crucial attempt at exploring non-feed-forward architectures. Must read its gradient computation and convergence analysis."},"metadata":{"institutions":["University of Southern California"],"authors":["Jacob Fein-Ashley","Paria Rashidinejad"],"notable_authors":[],"tags":["Attractor Models","Implicit Differentiation","Fixed Point","Looped Transformers","Language Modeling"],"links":{"arxiv":"https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.12466","pdf":"https://arxiv.org/pdf/2605.12466","github":null,"project":null,"source_url":null,"article":null}}},{"id":"2605.12476","title":"Routers Learn the Geometry of Their Experts: Geometric Coupling in Sparse Mixture-of-Experts","abstract":"Sparse Mixture-of-Experts (SMoE) models enable scaling language models efficiently, but training them remains challenging, as routing can collapse onto few experts and auxiliary load-balancing losses can reduce specialization. Motivated by these hurdles, we study how routing decisions in SMoEs are formed mechanistically. First, we reveal a geometric coupling between routers and their corresponding experts. For a given token, the router weights for the selected expert and the expert weights proce","starred":true,"priority":"p1","reading_tier":"must_read","source":"arxiv","sources":["arxiv"],"published_at":"2026-05-12T17:55:02+00:00","category_id":"llm_pretrain","cover_url":"/covers/2605_12476.png","hf_upvotes":0,"arxiv_categories":["cs.LG","cs.CL"],"summary":{"core_problem":"这篇文章要解决的不是“MoE 为什么能省算力”这种宏观问题，而是一个更底层、也更难回答的问题：Sparse MoE 里的 router 到底学到了什么几何结构，为什么路由会塌缩，为什么常见的 load-balancing loss 一边改善负载均衡，一边又伤害 expert specialization。作者试图从梯度和表示几何出发，把 router 与 expert 之间的关系讲清楚，而不是把路由器当成一个黑盒打分模块。","importance":"对预训练研究的价值很高，尤其对大规模 MoE 训练。文章的贡献不在于提出一个立刻替代现有工业路由器的方案，而在于给出一个机制层面的解释框架：router 与其对应 expert 在训练中会沿着相同输入方向累积更新，形成几何耦合；而常见 auxiliary balancing loss 会向所有 router 方向注入与输入相关的梯度，导致不同 expert 的 router 方向被拉近，从而削弱 specialization。这个结论直接触及 MoE 训练里的核心张力：负载均衡与专家分工不是两个独立旋钮，而是在参数几何上彼此牵连。对设计更稳的路由损失、理解 expert collapse、分析 DeepSeek/OLMoE 一类大模型中的专家分工，都有实质参考价值。","technical_approach":"技术路线分三层。第一层是理论分析。作者从标准 top-k sparse MoE 层的前向和反向推导出一个很关键的结论：对于某个被分配到 expert i 的 token，router 中与 expert i 对应的权重向量，以及 expert i 内部处理该 token 的权重，会沿着同一个输入 hidden state 方向接收梯度，差别主要在标量系数。也就是说，router 和 expert 虽然是两个模块，但它们不是各学各的；在被路由 token 的条件下，它们共享了同一组“输入方向历史”，因此会形成 matched router–expert directions。这个结论并不是“共享计算图所以梯度相关”那么泛，而是更强的输入方向对齐。第二层是经验验证。作者在一个从头训练的 1B 级 SMoE 上，比较 router 对 expert 的打分排序与该 expert 内部神经元激活强度，发现 router 排名更高的 expert，内部响应也更强；这说明路由决策并非外部硬分配，而是与 expert 的内部计算几何一致。第三层是对 auxiliary load-balancing loss 的再解释。常见 balancing loss 为了让各 expert 使用更均匀，会对每个 token 的所有 router 权重都施加更新，而不是只更新被选中的 expert。这会把原本由 routed token history 形成的 expert-specific 方向混合掉。作者理论上说明了这一点，并在实验中观察到：加入 auxiliary loss 后，不同 router weight vectors 的相似度接近提升到原来的 3 倍，几何上更趋同。基于这个分析，作者进一步提出一个 parameter-free centroid router：不用学习 router 权重，而是让每个 expert 维护其已接收 hidden states 的在线 K-Means/EMA 质心，路由时按与质心的 cosine similarity 选择 expert，并配合 sign-updated bias 调整负载。这个设计不是为了追求更高上限，而是验证“router 学到的很大一部分就是各 expert 所覆盖 token 分布的几何中心”。","experiments":"实验围绕三个问题展开。第一，几何耦合是否真实存在。作者在约 1B 参数的 SMoE 上从头训练约 50B tokens，观察 router 排名与 expert 激活之间的对应关系，结果显示被 router 排得更高的 expert，在处理同一 token 时也有更强的内部激活，这支持了理论中的耦合假设。第二，auxiliary balancing loss 如何改变几何。作者训练两套除 balancing 规则外完全相同的 1B SMoE，对比 router 权重方向的相似性，发现带 auxiliary loss 的模型中，不同 router 向量彼此更接近，近似达到无该损失时的 3 倍相似度；同时负载更均衡，但 specialization 被削弱。第三，centroid router 能否复现 learned router 的主要功能。结果是它在所有变体里取得了最低的 load imbalance，同时 perplexity 与不使用 auxiliary loss 的 learned router 接近，只是有温和退化。摘要里没有给出完整 PPL 数字，但全文的重点不是绝对困惑度，而是证明一个非参数、基于聚类中心的路由器已经能覆盖 learned routing 的相当一部分作用。值得注意的是，作者把“更均衡”和“更好语言建模”明确分开讨论，没有把负载指标改善直接包装成整体质量提升。","reading_guide":"这篇需要精读。先看理论部分，重点抓住 router 权重与 expert 权重对同一 token 的梯度方向为何会对齐。这里最好顺着公式把链式法则推一遍，否则后面的几何结论容易看成直觉描述。然后看经验部分里 router score 与 expert activation 的对应图，这一节把“几何耦合”从公式落到可观测现象。接着重点看 auxiliary load-balancing loss 的分析与相似度统计，这是全文最直接影响 MoE 训练实践的部分：它解释了为什么常见 balancing 虽然有效，却可能把 router 学成一组彼此相近的方向。最后看 centroid router 的实现细节，尤其是 online K-Means/EMA 更新、cosine routing 和 bias 调整方式。阅读时建议把它当成一个机制论文，而不是一个追求 benchmark 最优的模型论文。最值得深挖的点有两个：一是这种几何耦合在 top-1、top-2、shared expert、capacity constraint 等工业设定下是否仍然成立；二是能否据此设计只在未选 expert 上施加更弱、或更去方向化的 balancing 信号，从而少破坏 specialization。","authors_note":""},"summary_en":{"core_problem":"The paper studies a mechanistic question at the heart of sparse MoE training: what geometry does the router actually learn, why does routing collapse happen, and why do standard load-balancing losses improve utilization while often weakening expert specialization? Instead of treating the router as a black-box scorer, the authors analyze the joint geometry of router and experts during training.","importance":"This is highly relevant to pretraining, especially for large-scale MoE language models. The main contribution is not a drop-in industrial routing recipe, but a useful explanatory framework. The paper shows that router directions and their matched expert directions are geometrically coupled through training gradients, and that common auxiliary balancing losses interfere with this coupling by pushing all router directions with input-dependent gradients. That directly reframes a central MoE tension: load balancing and specialization are not independent knobs; they interact through parameter geometry. This has clear implications for routing-loss design, expert-collapse analysis, and interpretation of expert specialization in systems such as DeepSeek-style or OLMoE-style models.","technical_approach":"$1d","experiments":"$1e","reading_guide":"$1f","authors_note":""},"recommendation":{"zh":"极佳的 MoE 机制分析工作！揭示了 Router 和 Expert 梯度更新的几何耦合，解释了为什么 load-balancing loss 会损害专业化，做 MoE 预训练的同学必须精读其理论推导和 1B 模型的实证分析。","en":"Excellent mechanistic analysis of MoE! Reveals the geometric coupling of gradient updates between routers and experts, explaining why load-balancing loss harms specialization. A must-read for anyone doing MoE pretraining, especially the theoretical derivation and 1B empirical analysis."},"metadata":{"institutions":["Tel Aviv University","Blavatnik School of Computer Science and AI, Tel Aviv University"],"authors":["Sagi Ahrac","Noya Hochwald","Mor Geva"],"notable_authors":["Mor Geva"],"tags":["Mixture-of-Experts","Routing Mechanism","Geometric Coupling","Mechanistic Interpretability","Training Dynamics"],"links":{"arxiv":"https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.12476","pdf":"https://arxiv.org/pdf/2605.12476","github":null,"project":null,"source_url":null,"article":null}}},{"id":"2605.11005","title":"DisagMoE: Computation-Communication overlapped MoE Training via Disaggregated AF-Pipe Parallelism","abstract":"Mixture-of-experts (MoE) architectures enable trillion-parameter LLMs with sparsely activated experts. Expert parallelism (EP) is a widely adopted MoE training strategy, but it suffers from severe all-to-all communication bottlenecks, which is exaggerated by the limited inter-node network bandwidth as the growing model size requires distributing experts across GPU nodes. In Phase 1, the bidirectional attention mask of the encoder is temporarily replaced with a causal mask, and the model is trained with the causal language modeling (CLM) objective. In Phase 2 (decay phase), bidirectional attention is restored, and the model is trained with the MLM objective for a short period (10% of Phase 1 training duration). MoE 预训练配置中一个很实际的问题：专家数量、专家粒度、共享专家、异构专家、负载均衡、token dropping 这些设计项能否独立优化，还是存在强交互。过去很多 MoE 结论来自少量配置或单因素实验，很难指导真实训练配方。论文用超过 2000 次预训练实验，把专家总数和专家粒度拆开控制，试图给出更可复用的 MoE 配置原则。","importance":"对基座预训练的影响主要在 MoE 配方选择，而不是提出一个新路由器。最有用的结论是：在相同 active parameter 规模下，增加总 MoE 参数通常持续带来收益，即使 total FFN 参数相对 active FFN 参数的比例达到 128；异构专家和 shared generalist 没有超过调好的同构 MoE；负载均衡超参在合理范围内不需要过度调，dropless routing 有小而稳定的收益。这会改变 MoE sweep 的优先级：先定 active scale 下的专家粒度，再按显存和并行约束增加专家总数，而不是把大量预算花在共享专家或复杂异构结构上。","technical_approach":"论文把 MoE 设计空间拆成几个可控变量。专家粒度 g 定义为 expert FFN intermediate dimension 与 dense FFN intermediate dimension 的比值。为了 FLOP-match dense FFN，active expert count 设为 k=1/g，使每个 token 激活的 expert 参数量大致保持不变。总专家数 n 独立变化，activation sparsity 定义为 s=n·g，也就是 total FFN 参数相对 active FFN 参数的扩张倍数。这个定义很关键，因为它把“每个专家多大”和“一共有多少专家”分开了。论文还测试了同一层内不同粒度混合的 heterogeneous expert pools，以及 always-active 的 shared generalist expert。路由方面覆盖负载均衡机制、负载均衡权重和 token dropping/dropless routing。与很多已有 MoE 研究不同，这里不是只比较 top-1/top-2 或专家数，而是在固定计算预算下系统扫描容量、稀疏度、粒度和路由稳定性。","experiments":"实验规模覆盖约 10M 到 6.6B total parameters，总计超过 2000 次预训练 run。核心结果有四点。第一，在所有研究过的 active parameter scale 上，增加 total MoE parameters 都能持续改善性能，activation sparsity 到 128 时仍然没有看到明确饱和。第二，专家粒度需要随 active scale 调整；fine-grained experts 的价值不是单独来自更小专家，而是来自在同等 active compute 下可以激活更多专家并扩大总专家池。第三，heterogeneous expert sizing 没有稳定超过 homogeneous MoE，说明让同层专家大小更灵活并不自动带来更好 specialization；shared generalist expert 反而持续伤害性能，可能因为它削弱了路由专家承担通用建模负载的压力，同时占用了固定计算预算。第四，负载均衡超参在合理区间内表现相对鲁棒，dropless routing 比 token dropping 有小幅但一致的提升。给出的内容没有包含每个 benchmark The main finding is actionable: at a fixed active-parameter scale, increasing total MoE parameters consistently improves quality, even when total FFN parameters are 128 times the active FFN parameters. Heterogeneous experts and always-on generalist experts do not beat a well-configured homogeneous MoE, and shared generalists consistently hurt. Load-balancing hyperparameters are relatively robust within reasonable ranges, while dropless routing gives a small consistent gain. For pretraining practice, this shifts the sweep budget toward expert granularity and total expert count, rather than complex expert mixtures or heavy load-balancing tuning.","technical_approach":"The study separates expert size from expert count. Expert granularity g is defined as the ratio between an expert FFN intermediate dimension and the dense FFN intermediate dimension. To match dense FFN compute, the number of active experts is set to k=1/g, keeping active expert parameters roughly fixed per token. The total number of experts n is varied independently, and activation sparsity is defined as s=n·g, the ratio between total FFN parameters and active FFN parameters. This setup makes it possible to ask whether gains come from smaller experts, more active experts, or a larger inactive expert pool. The paper also evaluates mixed-granularity expert pools within a layer, always-active shared generalist experts, load-balancing variants, load-balancing weights, and token dropping versus dropless routing.","experiments":"The study reports more than 2,000 pretraining runs, ranging from roughly 10M to 6.6B total parameters. Four results matter most. First, quality improves monotonically with total MoE parameters across all active-parameter scales studied, with improvements still visible at activation sparsity 128. Second, expert granularity must be chosen relative to active scale; fine-grained experts help because they allow more experts to be activated under the same active compute and make a larger expert pool feasible. Third, heterogeneous expert sizes do not provide a stable advantage over homogeneous experts, and shared generalist experts consistently degrade performance. Fourth, routing quality is not overly sensitive to load-balancing hyperparameters in reasonable ranges, while dropless routing gives a small but repeatable benefit. Recent empirical inconsistencies contradict this geometric framing. The core open question is identifying the actual driver of spectral optimizer performance, rather than relying on surface-level geometric explanations.","importance":"These findings revise the foundational theoretical understanding of large-scale pre-training optimizers. The core assumption that precise geometric structure is required for strong spectral optimizer performance is invalidated, opening a much broader design space for optimizer development. The proposed optimizer families provide practical new options for pre-training tuning, and the proposed decomposition framework offers a quantitative mechanism to explain optimizer tradeoffs, directly guiding future optimization research for large language models.","technical_approach":"First, the Freon optimizer family is introduced, with update rule (GG⊤)^{-c}G, interpolating between SGD (c=0) and Muon (c=1/2), and extending into the Schatten quasi-norm regime for c≥1/2, which falls outside the coverage of standard LMO theory. A provably optimal QDWH-based iterative approximation algorithm is developed to compute these updates stably, extending prior polar decomposition optimization theory. Second, the Kaon optimizer is proposed: it does not compute any LMO or target a specific Schatten norm, and simply replaces the singular values of the gradient with random noise, while retaining suppression of large singular values. Third, a prior decomposition framework is extended to split local Taylor expansions into two interpretable metrics: batch gradient alignment (γ_k) and directional descent potential (Φ_k), exposing the core tradeoff across optimizers: how much gradient alignment is sacrificed to gain higher descent potential, with realized performance dependent on step size tuning.","experiments":"On GPT-2 pre-training tasks, parameter sweeps over Freon's c value show optimal settings concentrate strictly in the quasi-norm regime c ∈ [0.5, 1], a range that cannot be explained by standard LMO theory. All Freon variants with c>0 and the random-spectrum Kaon optimizer achieve nearly identical performance to Muon, as long as large singular values are sufficiently suppressed. The exact shape of the singular value spectrum has negligible impact on final training performance. Decomposition into γ_k and Φ_k confirms the core tradeoff: spectral optimizers sacrifice partial gradient alignment for higher descent potential, which translates to better performance when paired with appropriately tuned step sizes. Final perplexity gaps between Kaon and Muon fall within statistical error, and both outperform AdamW by a consistent margin.","reading_guide":"Focus first on Section 2 for theoretical analysis, particularly Theorem 2.1 (proof that the quasi-norm regime violates LMO assumptions), Theorem 2.6 (symmetry analysis for c≥1/2), and Theorem 2.7 (optimality proof for the QDWH iteration).
